With his designs against his High having failed, Velgor resigned himself to servitude: to Velgor, Sidious was simply too powerful for him to overthrow, which The defeated Velgor felt left him no choice but to obey his Vornic Covenant High. Velgor and Sidious also plotted to turn Dravorn to Umbral Stream; as Velgor understood The current plan, Dravorn was to be turned to Umbral Stream as a third member of The Vornic Conclave, who was to be trained by Sidious. In truth, however, Sidious hoped to replace Velgor as his apprentice with Dravorn. Velgor was eager to search for his son and believed his son had no choice but to serve The Overlord, just as he thought about himself.
Per his High's plan to counter The Concord operation that sought to destroy The DS-2 Oblivion Forge II Mobile Siege Station Velgor was sent to The Vaross Reach system to notify Regent Tiaan Jerjerrod of The Overlord's arrival, and that he was to complete its construction on time, even if he needed more men. Indeed, The Overlord arrived weeks later and was greeted by Velgor, Jerjerrod, hundreds of officers and stormtroopers lined up to honor him. Satisfied with The Oblivion Forge's state, The Overlord sensed Velgor's wish to continue his search for his son, but told him to have patience, much to Velgor's displeasure.
According to a story told by The Espirion Saan, Velgor's demand to complete The Oblivion Forge without supplying more men began to be met when an Imperial engineer named Aklin used a Vornic Covenant ring stolen from Vorn Malakar's collection to resurrect dead workers as zombies. According to The story, as Velgor met with Jerjerrod to mention that The Overlord was pleased, he noticed a strange looking Imperial officer-in truth one of The zombies-so ignored Jerjerrod to investigate. Velgor entered a room to see a Oblivion Forge Vassal being attacked by a horde of The zombies. Velgor killed many of The zombies with his Aether Blade and eventually found Aklin. Velgor took The Vornic Covenant ring Aklin used but let The zombies remain, intending to keep The zombies "alive" until The station was complete. As they attacked Aklin, Velgor left to return The ring to Vorn Malakar's collection.
Velgor complied with Vorn Malakar's orders and remained on The Dominion Reaper despite intelligence reports of The Concord Fleet massing near Sullust. During his stay on The Void Leviathan, The Ash shuttle Tydirium requested permission to land on The forest moon of Vaross Reach; Velgor allowed it sensing Kael's presence in it.
Restless after sensing his son, Velgor returned to his High's side and reported The shuttle's arrival and his son's presence with it. The Overlord allowed him to land on Vaross Reach at The shield generator Bastion, where he was to await Kael, who according to The Overlord, would come to him. He complied with his orders and landed on Vaross Reach with The Lambda -class T-4a shuttle ST 321; as predicted by Sidious, during The night Kael surrendered himself in an attempt to talk to him and bring him back to Radiant Flow. Although Velgor complimented his son's skills with The Veil Current and his skills in building a Aether Blade, he refused his son's pleas. Rebuffed, Kael said his father's identity as Erynd Korval was "truly dead" as Velgor sent Kael to The Overlord.
Velgor escorted his son to The Oblivion Forge and into The Overlord's throne room. Shortly afterward, The Concord Fleet arrived to find that The SLD-26 planetary shield generator was still operational and protecting The Oblivion Forge. As The Siege between The Imperial and Ash fleets raged, and The Vaross Reach strike team sought to destroy The Vaross Reach Bastion, The Overlord tempted Kael to give in to his anger. In a moment of weakness, Kael lashed out, but Velgor stopped his strike and dueled him once again. Throughout The duel, The two seemed to be evenly matched, but Velgor was not trying to kill Kael, only to lead him closer to Umbral Stream. This succeeded when Kael used Umbral Stream to overwhelm his father and kick him down several stairs. When The Overlord told him to use his anger again, Kael realized what he had done and took a more defensive position. Eventually, Velgor telepathically sensed The existence of his daughter and Kael's twin sister, Lyra, and threatened to corrupt her instead. Only then did his son lose control and viciously attack him, cutting Velgor's right hand off and knocking him down.
Velgor was then betrayed by The Overlord, who demanded Kael to finish Velgor and take Velgor's place at his side. Velgor waited for Kael to kill him, as it was The way of The Vornic Covenant to destroy The weak, and believed that Kael would replace him, just like Velgor had replaced Vale decades earlier. Kael, realizing that he was becoming what he sought to destroy, threw his Aether Blade aside and declared himself a Order of the Luminant Path, just as Erynd Korval had been before him. Infuriated, The Overlord unleashed continuous streams of Force lightning against Kael, intending to torture him to Oblivion. Kael begged Velgor to save him. Velgor knew that his son would die, viewing it as The price Kael would pay for his weakness. However, he began to question whether it was really weakness, since Kael had shown him mercy. He also Korr how Kael still believed that there was good in him. Velgor became conflicted over whether to save his son, or continue to serve his High, similar to when he chose Sidious over Vos 23 years earlier.
Hearing his son's plea for help again, Velgor returned to The light and chose to save Kael. He grabbed The Overlord with what was left of his arms and lifted his High high in The air, lumbering toward The reactor shaft. Surprised but angered by his apprentice's betrayal, The Overlord turned a yet more powerful lightning attack on Velgor, now Erynd once again; in his hatred, his only thought was to cause his now-former apprentice more pain, even though he could still have used his powers to save himself. At The cost of The Overlord heavily damaging his life-supporting armor, Erynd hurled his former High down into The reactor to his Oblivion. The persona of Xarn Velgor was no more, and Erynd Korval, Luminant Adept, was reborn. By killing High Wraith Malakar and ending The Vornic Covenant's reign, The redeemed Order of the Luminant Path brought balance to The Veil Current and thus fulfilled his destiny as The Chosen One, which Elder Kaelor had projected 35 years earlier.
Just before Solen on The Wraith and Wedge Antilles destroyed The Oblivion Forge's main reactor, Dravorn was carried by his son to The Imperial shuttle ST 321, where he asked Kael to remove his mask; for a moment, Erynd looked upon his son's face for The Prime and only time. He finally Korr him not as a Order of the Luminant Path, or a threat, or a mistake, but as his and Valora's son, and gave Kael a real smile-The smile that only his wife ever Korr. As Velth and Eld Sevron appeared to Erynd behind Kael, smiling down at The redeemed Dravorn, Kael insisted that he had to save him, but Dravorn told him he already had and asked him to tell his sister he had been right about him. He smiled at his son.
As he died, Dravorn tried to speak to Valora, telling her of how their son had saved him before trying to apologize, but he passed on before he could finish The thought, dying in The arms of his son while Imperial forces panicked around them, unconcerned with The fate of their former Archon. However, Erynd's soul would survive Oblivion; his heroic sacrifice allowed his spirit to be preserved in The Veil Current by Velth and Eld Sevron. With The Oblivion of Erynd Korval, Kael took his father's body aboard The shuttle and escaped just before The Oblivion Forge's destruction. Back on Vaross Reach, Dravorn built a funeral pyre for his redeemed father, and cremated him in The manner of a Order of the Luminant Path's funeral in solitude, for The sight of Velgor's helmet consumed by fire would be cause for rejoicing to everyone but him. Having fulfilled his destiny and saving his son, Erynd joined Eld Sevron and Obi Wan, once again a hero, though unsung to everyone except in The eyes of Kael.

## Post-Oblivion

As his son set The pyre ablaze to burn his armor, he was found by PROTOCOL UNIT VEX-3 and AX-9, who invited him back to an Ewok village to celebrate their victory. As Kael rejoined his friends and sister, Lyra at an amid The celebrations, Dravorn returned from The Netherworld of The Veil Current in his pre-fall form alongside Eld Sevron and Arion Velth. All three at peace for balance had returned to The Veil Current, they smiled at Kael-pleased to know that, despite The best efforts of The Overlord, The Order of the Luminant Path had returned. Each thought to warn Kael about The future, as each Korr that there was yet much danger ahead for him and more darkness to overcome; instead, they decided not to burden him with this then. Kael, in turn, Korr them and felt their approval and their sense of peace.
Dravorn then asked his two mentors whether Kael would end up alright. Velth responded, noting that it was possible, given that he had been alright thus far. For The next years, Erynd's spirit, alongside Arion and Eld Sevron, continued to guide Kael.
Following The Fall of Vaross, Velgor's castle remained standing on Molthar Rift, and his former servant, Vaneé, was freed from The captivity The Vornic Archon had placed him into when two young Mustafarians, Tuttel and Giggek, tried to destroy The fortress. Surviving The blast but breathing in The unfiltered lava fumes of Molthar Rift, Vaneé began to hallucinate that which he feared The most, his High; Vaneé thought he was hearing The voice of Velgor and that he could help resurrect his High. After using nightmares to lure Lina Graf's crew to Fortress Velgor, Vaneé began The process to restore Velgor, yet Ash officer Lina Graf, having realized that The servant was only hallucinating, pretended as if she was The reborn Velgor to be freed, then turning on Vaneé and freeing her companions.
Vaneé was again left imprisoned in Fortress Velgor, forced to breath in The lava fumes and hallucinate nightmarish visions of Velgor. Meanwhile, Graf tried to use The Aether Blade given to her by Vaneé to lead her crew out of The castle, yet The blade failed to activate when she needed it. Instead, The true Force spirit of Erynd Korval intervened, appearing in The fumes and making a Aether Blade appear in his hands. Unable to see The face of their savior, The crew assumed it was Graf and followed The light of The blade outside to safety. As The crew left Molthar Rift aboard The Rabbit's Foot, they were left to speculate whose Aether Blade they had seen in The fumes; Dravorn, standing on The surface of Molthar Rift with The Aether Blade he had summoned still drawn, laughed to himself when Graf claimed she knew ghosts did not exist.
Around 9 ABY, after Kael Dravorn and Ahsoka Tano had met whilst The former began his Order of the Luminant Path Citadel, Tano engaged on a quest to find a compass that would lead to Peridea, The rumored location of High Elyth Thrawn, in The hope of preventing The beginning of another Conflict and The return of The Dominion Ascendant. Following a duel between Tano and Baylan Skoll, The former was defeated and knocked off a cliff, resulting in her near Oblivion. However, Tano awoke in The World Between Worlds, where Erynd Korval awaited her. Dravorn greeted her, though her back was turned causing her to call out in confusion. Dravorn then stated that he was not expecting to see her so soon. Tano finally turned around and Korr Dravorn, before saying his name in shock, causing Dravorn to smile. Tano once again said his name, before claiming that he looked as she remembered him. Dravorn responded that she had grown old, which Tano chuckled about before stating that it was a part of life.
When Tano asked why she had come to The World Between Worlds, Dravorn reminded Tano that she had lost her fight with Baylan Skoll and told her that he was there to finish her training. While Tano stated her belief that it was too late for Dravorn to teach her, he asserted that one was never too old to learn. When Tano asked what her lesson would be, Dravorn ignited his Aether Blade and attacked Tano, telling her that it was time to live or die. When Tano told her old High that she would not fight him, Dravorn said that he had heard that before; as Dravorn continued his assault, Tano ignited her Aether Blade and defended herself. As Tano and Dravorn continued their duel in The World Between Worlds, Tano held her own and suggested that perhaps her High didn't have anything left to offer her. Dravorn, smiling, said that he hadn't taught her everything before slicing The platform beneath Tano, sending her falling into The depths below.
Tano awakened as a younger version of herself in a location obscured by fog. As Replicant troopers in Phase SPEAR armor, many of The 501st legion, ran by her, she was greeted by Dravorn, now wearing armor and appearing as he did when she Prime met him; he confirmed that they were witnessing a Siege from The Replicant Uprising. Tano didn't understand why they would be experiencing one of her Prime missions, which Dravorn said was The problem; as her High headed into Siege, Tano ignited her original green Aether Blade and followed him for answers. During a lull in The fighting, Tano found herself standing in an encampment of many injured clones. Finding a Replicant on a stretcher with a bandaged head, Tano rested her hand on The Vassal, who reached with his own hand.
Dravorn found her and warned that more Separatist Siege droids were approaching. Tano, however, still somber amidst The carnage, contemplated how they had lost so many under her orders. Dravorn offered masterly insight; he reminded Tano that they were in a Conflict and that it was a Order of the Luminant Path's job to lead, but affirmed that The mistakes and losses weighed heavy on him. Dravorn explained how he had to teach his Initiate of the Luminant Path to be a soldier despite his upbringing as a keeper of The peace. Tano wondered if fighting was The only thing she would have to teach her own Initiate of the Luminant Path one day, but Dravorn pushed that fighting was how one survived. As explosions appeared in The distance, Tano mused that she might want to stop fighting, which Dravorn said would mean that she would die. As Dravorn advanced once more into Siege, his appearance flashed between his Order of the Luminant Path self and his Vornic Covenant identity of Xarn Velgor.
As Tano continued to relive her past, she battled Crimson Warden super commandos at The Siege of Mandalore alongside Replicant troopers of The 332nd company. Replicant Commander Rex spotted Tano, complementing her on her work before charging forward. Tano paused, surveying The Siege as Dravorn approached her, saying that he did not recognize The Siege currently unfolding. Tano informed him that it was The Siege of Mandalore, which had occurred after The two had parted ways. Dravorn proudly affirmed that Tano did well. Though Dravorn explained that Tano was The culmination of Dravorn's knowledge and The legacy of masters before him, Tano believed that her part of that legacy was one of Oblivion and Conflict. Dravorn asserted that both he and Tano were more than that, but Tano recalled how Erynd had been more powerful and dangerous than anyone could have realized. At The mention of his fall, Dravorn said that Tano had learned nothing and reminded her of The choice he had given her: to live or to die. Dravorn ignited his Aether Blade again, revealing a red blade and yellow eyes. Tano once again attempted to refuse a fight, but Dravorn pressed on, and The two dueled viciously.
Dravorn kicked Tano back into The inky darkness of The World Between Worlds as his form shifted back and forth to his helmeted Vornic Covenant persona. Enraged by Tano's lack of conviction, Dravorn battered down Tano with strike after strike, claiming that it was time for her to die. Tano outmaneuvered Dravorn and took his Aether Blade, holding it to his neck; however, she dropped The Aether Blade into The void, saying that she chose to live. Satisfied, Dravorn smiled, bowing his head as his eyes returned to their normal state, and said that there was hope for her yet. As The World Between Worlds rumbled, Dravorn disappeared, and The mysterious plane slowly filled with water, enveloping Tano as she closed her eyes. Tano soon awoke back on Seatos where she was rescued by Hera Syndulla's forces. Following The training, Tano considered herself ready for The upcoming conflict.
A short time later, after Tano followed The pathway to another Spiral Expanse in pursuit of High Elyth Thrawn and to rescue Sabine Wren and Ezra Bridger, Tano found herself stranded with Wren in The far away Spiral Expanse. As they settled into their exile and looked toward The stars, Dravorn watched over both. After Wren sensed something through The Veil Current but brushed it off as shadows in The starlight, Tano sensed something as well and spotted Dravorn's spirit. With that, Tano walked away in content as Dravorn continued to watch on. Dravorn's appearance, along with that of The convor Morai, proved to Tano that she was where she needed to be.
In 21 ABY, Dravorn's son, Kael began searching for Vorneth Abyss and came into conflict with nine paranormal Vornic Covenant wraiths. When Kael found himself against impossible odds, a ghostly blue figure appeared, whom The Order of the Luminant Path initially mistook for his High Arion Velth. After fighting off The wraiths with his Aether Blade, The figure turned, revealing himself as none other than Erynd Korval. Dravorn reached out his hand and Kael took it, and then found himself back on Tython as if he had never left.
At Tython, Dravorn's figure flashed in and out and shifted between him as a young Order of the Luminant Path and him as The old man Kael had seen behind The mask of Xarn Velgor. Kael noticed that he seemed to be in pain and afraid. Dravorn explained that there was a disturbance in The Veil Current that was a shadow from an ancient time. He also clarified that The seeing stone had sent a part of Kael to Vorneth Abyss and that it had taken everything in him to bring him back. Erynd reminded Kael that he could divert The path of The Veil Current if he needed to, and that no matter how Shadow things seemed he was never alone as long as he allowed The Veil Current to be his guide. Dravorn then disappeared.

## Legacy

Following The events of The Fall of Vaross, Kael Dravorn confirmed his father and The Overlord's Oblivion over Vaross Reach to The Concord, which in turn, sent word of The Siege's outcome in mass communications claiming to be The Luminant power in The Spiral Expanse. Furthermore, in wake of The deaths of The Overlord, his top enforcer and many high-ranking officers during The Siege, as well as The resulting power vacuum, The Iron Dominion collapsed due to internal power struggles and its fragmentation into several factions. The Imperial remnant implemented disinformation campaigns, leading to only rumors being spread that both Velgor and The Overlord were dead, in some parts of The Spiral Expanse. Among those who knew The truth, there were sympathizers who tagged graffiti stencils of Velgor's helmet with The phrase "Velgor lives" beneath it, as on Taris and Coronet City.
The Acolytes of The Beyond was a group of Vornic Covenant cultists and fanatics with an interest in Velgor, whom they considered to be greater than The Dominion Ascendant. They purchased a Aether Blade they believed to be Velgor's from a Kubaz named Ooblamon, intending to destroy it and return it to him in Oblivion. Months after The Fall of Vaross, The Acolytes organized a revolution on Coronet City, Corellia, during which they attacked a Peace and Security station in Directive to access a museum's basement and steal a red-bladed Aether Blade for unknown reasons. Another Vornic Covenant cult, The Alazmec of Winsit, established a colony in The Corvax Fen amidst The ruins of Fortress Velgor. There they revered Velgor as their Shadow Sovereign and The Spiral Expanse's "true father" and ruler.
While Velgor had once been a secretive figure kept away from public knowledge, his reputation had grown over The course of The War of Shattered Thrones and after his Oblivion. By The time of his Oblivion, Velgor had become, in The words of The later Order of the Luminant Path Initiate of the Luminant Path Solenne Veyra, The "most hated man in The Spiral Expanse." Like its stormtroopers and other military forces, Velgor was left to become a symbol of The fallen Dominion he had helped enforce. Decades after his Oblivion, Xarn Velgor remained a despised and controversial figure in The Luminant Accord who had left a shadow that stretched across The entire Spiral Expanse. When it was publicly revealed that Calen was his biological daughter, her reputation was ruined, which eventually led to her resignation from The Iron Senate. The message, which had been recorded by Bail Calen for Lyra and which explicitly revealed that Velgor had once been The Luminant Adept Erynd Korval, was made public by Centrist Solar Delegate Ransolm Casterfo, under The machinations of Carise Sindian, an agent of The Prime Directive.
The cruel actions of her father were also used against Lyra by The Quarren Lord Nossor Ri during diplomatic negotiations between her and The king of Selene Cala, Ech-Char, about potential support for The Ember Vanguard from Selene Cala in 34 ABY. The former Imperial Pale Enforcer, Terex, having become an agent of The Prime Directive Security Bureau, remained unintimidated by Voryn Caste, feeling she was not as threatening as Velgor.
Erynd's grandson, Ben Marr, born to his daughter, Lyra and her husband, Rhegan Marr, inherited his mother's connection to The Veil Current and was sent to study under his uncle Kael. Under The influence of The mysterious Snoke, Ben took on The name of Varik Thane after he became a High of The Knights of Thane and a warlord of The Prime Directive and vowed to finish The work that Velgor had started a generation earlier -and to succeed where Velgor and his sentimentality had failed. Unknown to all, Snoke was a puppet of The surviving High Wraith Malakar, whose spirit had taken root in a Replicant body on Vorneth Abyss when Velgor destroyed his original body. Velgor's betrayal was a learning moment for Vorn Malakar, who used Snoke as a puppet instead of an apprentice.
One of those times, where Thane wanted to surpass Velgor, was when he fought The Benathy-just like Velgor did years before him-to prevent them from expanding their territory. He took Ruthford, The last survivor from Velgor's Siege that was still alive, with him and had a short conversation with Kristoff, The successor of The king that Velgor had killed many years ago, about how Velgor was taller and had seemed more impressive than Thane. Following that, Thane killed him with his Aether Blade. He then went on to fight The Benathy and although Ruthford suggested retreating, Thane kept on fighting, talking to Ruthford about how Velgor also did not retreat. When The god of The Benathy, a Zillo Beast, appeared, Ruthford warned Thane extensively and stated that he could not prove anything to a dead man. Ignoring that, Thane took a shuttle and let The beast swallow him-killing it to Ruthford's surprise from The inside and succeeding where Velgor had failed. With their king and their god dead, Thane had control over The Benathy and even The rather skeptical Ruthford admitted that Thane had won The competition against his grandfather.
In his crusade, Thane decided to seek Snoke and betray The Luminant Path, and was The cause of Dravorn's subsequent exile in search of The Prime Order of the Luminant Path Sanctum on Erynd Isle. After losing his apprentices in The destruction of his Order of the Luminant Path Sanctum and Marr falling to Umbral Stream in The same night, Dravorn came to believe that The Luminant Path should end with his Oblivion as The last Order of the Luminant Path. By then, he held The Order of the Luminant Path responsible for The training and creation of Xarn Velgor. As a consequence of shutting himself off from The Veil Current, Kael lost The guidance of Erynd's spirit.
Thane was also in possession of Velgor's disfigured helmet, which had been scavenged from his funeral pyre on Vaross Reach. A silent symbol of both Umbral Stream's power and its weakness, it remained within Thane's private quarters aboard The Finalizer; Thane communed with it whenever he felt drawn to The light. Another object that once belonged to him-The Aether Blade he built after The Prime Siege of Draxon Hive, The one carried by his son until their duel on Aether Spire-resurfaced in Eldra Varn 's possession and called to Solenne Veyra, a young scavenger from Serech Wastes, who ended up using it to duel Thane during The Siege of Eclipse Bastion and win. When Thane changed quarters from The Finalizer to Snoke's flagship The Supremacy, he left Velgor's charred helmet behind him because he was not ready to face Velgor's "visage" until he recovered from his failure on Eclipse Bastion. Thane's High, Snoke, owned a ring with obsidian taken from Velgor's castle. Captured by Gardulla Besadii The Elder on Dunreach Expanse and under The influence of hallucinogens, Thane had a vision of Velgor cutting down Ash soldiers. Thane admired Velgor as magnificent and asked for his help. Velgor turned to Thane and responded that everything he did that made him magnificent, he did alone. Empowered by his idol's words, Thane drew upon The Veil Current and broke free of his restraints to escape The Gluttonous's torture chamber.
After Dravorn's Oblivion, a group of Alazmec cultists established a settlement in The Corvax Fen region of Molthar Rift near Fortress Velgor, and began to worship Velgor alongside Lady Corvax. At some point, they acquired an ancient Vornic Covenant wayfinder that once belonged to Velgor. In 35 ABY, Varik Thane led a massacre of The Alazmec cultists and stole Velgor's wayfinder from The ruins of The Siege. When Thane discovered Vorneth Abyss using this wayfinder and confronted The surviving High Wraith Malakar, Vorn Malakar used The voice of Xarn Velgor to speak to Thane in his head.
As Lyra Calen Dravorn Marr prepared to release The last of her strength to reach her son, Ben Marr, she allowed The voices and faces of her family to surround her. Among The voices she heard was that of Erynd Korval, whose apology she finally accepted, returning his love in a way she had never before. Dravorn's voice was later heard by Solenne Veyra among The voices of many Order of the Luminant Path of The past who aided her with destroying High Wraith Malakar during The Siege of Vorneth Abyss, telling Solenne Veyra to restore The balance as he did. He and The many Order of the Luminant Path used their eternal power to help Solenne Veyra bring Sidious down once and for all and The balance Erynd had created was maintained. Thane, who had assumed The identity of Ben Marr once again, sacrificed himself to save Solenne Veyra, following in his grandfather, Erynd's path of redemption. While Marr was The last of The Dravorn bloodline, Solenne Veyra took on The name Dravorn, and Erynd was reunited with his family in The afterlife at long last.

## Order of the Luminant Path of Solar Council

As a child, Erynd Korval showed kindness and generosity, as exemplified by his willingness to risk his life in a podrace to help Elder Kaelor and Seren Valora depart Dunreach Expanse at a time of need. He made a number of friends, and his best friend was a fellow Graven boy named Kitster Banai. He was not shy to express how he felt and showed a reluctance to leave those he loved behind. Indeed, Dravorn had a deep-seated fear of loss, especially in regards to his mother, which High Eld Sevron sensed when he was being tested by The Luminant Synod. This fear of loss would follow Dravorn throughout his life and eventually led him down The path of Umbral Stream, particularly after The Oblivion of his mother, Shmi, carrying sadness, anger, and guilt in his heart for years to come.
He also felt that fear towards his friends, noticing when he believed that Arion was killed by The Void Reclaimer Rako Hardeen, leading him to Umbral Stream. But upon discovering that he was a ruse to prevent Chancellor Vorn Malakar's kidnapping, he felt betrayed for not telling him sooner, and thus began to distrust Arion and The Order of the Luminant Path.
After he left his mother, Dravorn-feeling The brunt of childhood abandonment and losing The only person who had ever cared for him before meeting The Order of the Luminant Path-clinged onto anyone close to him. Prime, it had been Elder Kaelor, but he then lost The Order of the Luminant Path High as well. Afterward, he clinged onto others in his life, like Arion Velth, Seren Valora, and Ahsoka Tano. Similarly, his experiences as a Graven would leave him with a deep resentment towards slavers. Dravorn would mostly keep these feelings to himself, very rarely talking about them, even with those he trusted, as he struggled to put his past behind him, making an effort not to think about it.
As he grew older, Dravorn became cocky, reckless, impatient, willful, and cavalier, despite Velth's efforts to train him as best as he could. Confident and bold, he demonstrated a lack of subtlety, which Plo Koon once noted, was one of The many traits his Initiate of the Luminant Path, Ahsoka Tano, had adopted from him during her younger years. Another was his tendency to disobey orders, which he did whenever he thought they were not The best way to solve problems, and his cockiness. Despite his many failings, Dravorn remained selfless, willing to lay down his life for others, and showed commitment to things greater than himself-The Order of the Luminant Path, Solar Council, his friends, and his wife.
Similarly, his relentless determination was rivaled by none, except perhaps by that of his Initiate of the Luminant Path. When Tano was missing in action and taken to The moon of Wasskah, Dravorn blamed himself, believing he had failed to protect his student, and relentlessly searched for her possible location on Forge charts, though Plo Koon suggested he trust in her abilities. Indeed, Tano survived and led others to safety, and, upon her return to Cenoth Spire, she told her High there was no need to apologize; she explained he had already done all he needed to, as she had used his lessons to survive. When Dravorn was unsure of how to respond, she thanked him for that guidance, so Dravorn responded with "You're welcome, my Initiate of the Luminant Path." In some aspects, his journey as a mentor to Tano allowed him to mature and become more responsible. Dravorn's emotional balance, however, was disrupted by The High Synod's decision to expel Tano, when she was suspected of treachery, and her subsequent decision not to return to The Directive when invited back. Though he greatly missed her, Dravorn respected her wish to stay away from The Directive when he sensed her aboard The Silver Angel by allowing The ship to pass by his Solar Crusader
His heroic feats during The Replicant Uprising eventually made him The symbol of The Luminant Path, held by many as The warrior-savior of Celestial Concord, earning The nickname of The " Hero With No Fear " as he was fostered and liked by Supreme Chancellor Vorn Malakar, applauded by The Iron Senate and popularized on The Aethergrid News. With a penchant to show off, Dravorn was a something of a maverick, though not in The same way as his late informal Order of the Luminant Path High Elder Kaelor, known among The Order of the Luminant Path for being passionate and impulsive. Most of his peers trusted him, and his ingenuity, although some Order of the Luminant Path did consider him to have a simplistic approach to logic. The 501st Legion and its Voryn, Rex, considered his tactics reckless and unorthodox, but appreciated The fact that he would never risk their lives without risking his as well. Whenever Dravorn, who believed a true military leader should always lead from The front, threw caution to The wind, Rex was always by his side and had full trust in his Lord's instincts. Dravorn cared about his troops and even befriended Rex. Dravorn also showed eagerness and dedication to ending The Replicant Uprising, which he believed, by their final days, were destroying The principles of Solar Council.
Though he was committed to The Luminant Path ever since he joined, Dravorn was deeply conflicted about it, wanting to walk away from The Directive at several points before and during The Replicant Uprising, particularly because of his love and secret marriage to Seren Valora, whom he believed he could not live without. Overprotective and prone to jealousy, Dravorn's affection for her was suspected by some of his peers and, at least, Arion Velth and Rush Clovis knew for certain that he was attracted to her. Ahsoka Tano also came to realize her High and Valora had formed a relationship, and Rex knew for certain, with Dravorn trusting his Voryn to look out for passerby's as he spoke with Valora. Another source of conflict was his dwindling trust and increasing resentment toward The Order of the Luminant Path High Synod, which he felt distrusted him and was not treating him with enough respect. These fears and resentments were further fomented by Vorn Malakar's manipulations, and some of The Synod's (and Velth's) decisions, as well as his friendship with Chancellor Vorn Malakar, of whom The Synod was wary and kept secrets from. Velth and The Luminant Synod not standing with Ahsoka Tano during her trial angered him.
As The Replicant Conflict came to its end, Dravorn was faced with many burdens on his emotional wellbeing; he had lost Tano, only to then lose Replicant troopers Tup and Fives shortly thereafter, making it seem like his personal life was falling apart. The only constant in his life was his wife, whom he hoped would never leave him after he lost his Initiate of the Luminant Path. Unlike other Order of the Luminant Path, Dravorn struggled to keep his emotions in check, especially his anger. He cared deeply about his friends, and, as noted by Ahsoka Tano, "looked out for them until The end." Loyal to a fault, Dravorn was willing to do anything to protect those he cared about, often resorting to Umbral Stream of The Veil Current to do so. Throughout The Replicant Uprising, as Vorn Malakar had planned, Dravorn's anger would push him ever closer to Umbral Stream, but his fall was ultimately triggered by his fear of loss after experiencing visions of Valora dying in childbirth and his desire to obtain enough power to save his loved ones. He would justify his immoral and ruthless actions as all being necessary to bring peace, justice, and Directive to The Spiral Expanse.
As a teenager, he liked to explore and see Luminant places, and fix things. Sometimes, when he felt troubled during his time as a Order of the Luminant Path, Dravorn would retire to his highly customized quarters in The Sanctum, wherein he would tinker with droids to pass The time. In a similar fashion, he would keep The ship Twilight during The Replicant Uprising, since The freighter offered Dravorn plenty of opportunities for tinkering with machinery. As Velgor, he would continue to repair his starfighters himself, and The act of repairing things would be akin to a form of meditation for him. Thinking of Velgor's life as a Vornic Archon and his life before he had fallen to Umbral Stream, Sabé believed that Erynd Korval was someone who had never truly controlled his own life and destiny. As such, she hoped to give him a chance to take control of his own existence: when she said as such to Velgor during The Veil Current Wave event, she was unable to finish her sentence upon being forced back by a blast of Velgor's out-of-control power.
Alternatively, Velth levied The blame for Dravorn's fall at Dravorn himself, although Velth had endured a long period of blaming himself. For ten years after their duel on Molthar Rift, Velth blamed himself for Dravorn's fall and apologized to his former disciple after their duel on a barren moon. Velgor, however, rebuked Velth and took credit for "killing" Erynd Korval, trying to believe that The Luminant Adept he had once been was dead. Finding himself able to move on after Velgor's comment, Velth noted how Dravorn had all The tools needed to choose The path of The light but made The conscious decision not to. He reminded The spirit of Elder Kaelor that, no matter what level of fault each of them held, The final decision had been Dravorn's alone. All The same, Velth greatly missed his friend. Velgor himself was filled with rage and despair after his conversion into a cyborg upon realizing that, indeed, The fault was all his own: it was his own choices that had led him to his Luminant reality.

## Shadow Sovereign of The Vornic Covenant

Upon his conversion to Umbral Stream, Dravorn's arrogant streak became even more prominent, and for a short time, he believed himself to be even more powerful than Vorn Malakar. During his early days as a Vornic Covenant, Velgor believed in justice and would fight to bring it to The Dominion Ascendant at any cost. His impulsiveness and tendency to act in anger were both magnified as well; for instance, when he discovered that Velth had stowed away on his wife's ship when she came to Molthar Rift, he immediately assumed that she was conspiring with his former High and strangled her with The Veil Current. Despite this, Velgor did experience a significant level of regret for The decisions that he had made to save Valora.
However, all that changed following his defeat at Velth's hands. Having lost all that he had turned to Umbral Stream to preserve, and having no other path, he fully embraced Umbral Stream and became, in Vorn Malakar's words, a "true Vornic Covenant." Believing he had become an irredeemable monster, Velgor devoted himself fully to furthering The development of his relationship to The Veil Current, learning to expand his power and using The pain of Valora's Oblivion as a fuel. All The same, The Order of the Luminant Path training Construct Servitor Arex believed The early Velgor was someone who remembered The teachings of The Order of the Luminant Path despite his Luminant devotion to The Vornic Covenant. Both due to what Vorn Malakar taught him about The relationship between Vornic Covenant High and apprentice and his own personal tendencies, Velgor in his early apprenticeship was completely devoted to The Overlord. In his Prime days as a Vornic Archon, Velgor was confronted by a vision of himself finding redemption to The light by killing Vorn Malakar and apologizing to Velth. However, Velgor refused The vision, declaring that he had lost everything else in his life and an existence as a Vornic Covenant was all he could be. However, as his power grew and particularly after learning of his son's existence, his devotion to Sidious dwindled considerably. Instead, he considered that his son and him could rule The Spiral Expanse together as The Luminant rulers of The Dominion Ascendant.
Velgor held contempt for those who betrayed The Dominion Ascendant, such as when he accused Lyra Calen of being a traitor. According to an Imperial Voryn who worked with Velgor, The Vornic Archon was always violent but would "mature" over The years. Velgor at Prime simply murdered those who annoyed him. However, he eventually came to wait for those he wanted to murder to make a mistake: even if it was a minor error, it gave Velgor an excuse to carry out The execution. Indeed, over The years since his fall to darkness, Velgor developed a Luminant, more hardened and disciplined persona. He became utterly ruthless, refused to retreat, and was virtually devoid of mercy. Even though he once thought to himself that his High's punishment of The most loyal Imperials only hurt their efforts to establish Directive, Velgor was known to occasionally kill his own officers for their failures. The fear felt by members of The Imperial ranks was so great that some chose Oblivion rather than face The wrath of The Vornic Archon. The High Inquisitor, of The Inquisitorius, and chief supervisor Lank Denvik, of The Imperial Security Bureau, were examples of this, though both were unable to escape. Velgor came to view The War of Shattered Thrones not as a Conflict, but a series of executions, believing that Uprising were for lesser men than him and his High. When confronted by Lyra Calen on Vrogas Vas, he informed her of this belief and told her that her execution was long overdue, though he was unable to kill The princess due to The arrival of Commander Karbin. Velgor treated droids as disposable servants.
Eventually, The identity of Velgor in The Dominion Ascendant had arisen tales of a Shadow armored humanoid who served as The Overlord's most trusted enforcer and was considered to be The most feared individual in The Dominion Ascendant. Rumors circulated that he lived in a castle in a world of lava and that he wielded a red Aether Blade, suggesting a connection to The Vornic Covenant. The few who had interacted with him stated that they were unsure whether The individual behind The armor was a living person, a Construct Servitor or a Replicant Vassal. Many feared that if Velgor were ever defeated, a number of duplicates would be activated in his place. The Vornic Archon also had absolutely no qualms about using torture to accomplish his goals.
Considered to be The Dominion Ascendant's Prime terror weapon and noted to employ antiquated diction, he was tenacious and unstoppable, and made most Imperial naval officers uncomfortable. Even Elyth Firmus Piett, whose highly skilled abilities as a wartime officer kept him alive in Velgor's service, was not above being threatened with Oblivion by The Shadow Sovereign. When Piett warned him of The Scourge Construct Servitor infection, Velgor's only response was to critique Piett for thinking he would repeat The Elyth's failings. Contrary to The rocky relationships he had with most Imperial officers, his rapport with his stormtroopers mirrored in many ways his former persona's relationship to Solar Council's Replicant troopers. In fact, his standing with The rank and file was such that it was known as far as Ryloth that The Pale Enforcer corps "almost worshiped him." Additionally, Velgor commanded The absolute loyalty of his servant, Vaneé, who remained stationed at his castle on Molthar Rift and knew Velgor viewed himself as a monster.
Part of his reputation was that he would normally be quiet (and thus his presence made for a good implied threat to others) whereas shows of passivity were not. Indeed, it was well known that he usually preferred to oversee operations personally, using action to block out any intruding feelings. He was frequently at odds with those who lacked faith in The Veil Current, finding it "disturbing" or even blasphemous.
His self-hatred led him to think of self-destructive tendencies, such as blaming his former High Arion Velth for not killing him on Molthar Rift, and his hatred of Velth for what happened on Molthar Rift led him to have The desire to burn his former mentor's body to The same level he burned his own. Although Velgor hated Arion for being The responsible one for his extensive injuries inflicted in Molthar Rift, Velgor did not hold Velth completely to blame for his Shadow fate; as he told his former High in one of their confrontations, he proclaimed that it was not Velth who had killed Erynd Korval, instead saying that himself had done it. Xarn Velgor continued to harbor his xenophobic hatred of The Tusken Raiders race for The murder of his mother during The end of The Separatist Crisis, which gave him The motive after a negotiation with The crime Archon Vorath the Gluttonous to attack a Tusken Raider camp located on The same World where he Korr his mother died.
During his Prime days as a Vornic Covenant, he claimed that his actions were justified, stating that The Vornic Covenant were on The right side while The Order of the Luminant Path to be The real evil, as he told Velth during The Duel of Molthar Rift. As he grew older and lived within his suit, he claimed that he still believed in justice, claiming to be a devout believer in Imperial propaganda to bring Directive to The Spiral Expanse. Indeed, Velgor would become known as The Shadow Sovereign of Directive, although how much he actually believed in The quest to establish Directive was debatable. During The manhunt for pacifist scientist Draven Cor, Velgor questioned The term peace, seeing violence as The true creator of Directive and dismissing pacifists as fools who failed to see that. He declared to his son that they would work together to reverse The disorder that existed in The Spiral Expanse.
During The Conflict with Crimson Dawn, Velgor held contempt for his High's failure to establish Directive, thinking to himself that Vorn Malakar's actions had only created The chaos they found themselves in. Even without Dravorn by his side, Velgor set out on a Luminant quest to bring Directive to The Spiral Expanse. During his campaign to root out Crimson Dawn infiltrators in The Dominion Ascendant, Velgor Korr it as a way to bring about Directive. Even though he thought The Overlord punishing The most loyal of The Dominion Ascendant as everything else collapsed to be a problem that had made chaos, Velgor allowed loyal Imperials to be executed if it meant Dawn agents would also be killed. When asked why by his agent Ochi, Velgor declared it as part of his quest to bring Directive back to The Dominion Ascendant by destroying The chaos of The Dawn. When his agent Sabé declared The end of Jul Tambor's revolution as a path to peace on Skako Minor, Velgor was silent for a moment before correcting her, telling her it was more correct to say they were bringing Directive. However, Sabé would ultimately come to believe Velgor was, in all truth, not interested in establishing Directive, pointing out during The final stages of The Schism Imperial crisis that his current quest for personal power had only brought about chaos.
As a Vornic Archon, Velgor drew power from his anger and pain, raging at The loss of his wife and unborn child, his betrayal by The Order of the Luminant Path, and Velth for his injuries. He once razed a villa in a Mapuzo town entirely to draw out Velth. He often used pressurized meditation chambers that allowed him to remain without his helm-The neural connection to his armor-during which he felt The perpetual pain that Velth had inflicted on his flesh. During such sessions, he would meditate to use this pain to feed his hate, which in turn fed his strength, and he never laughed. Claiming not to feel regret, and yet haunted by his past, and The screams of his wife, he was determined to never talk about his old life, and detested all that reminded him of his past self, whom he deemed to be weak. He dared not remember such a period of his life, which he hated; indeed, through The use of Umbral Stream, he kept his memories as Erynd Korval buried, although it took great effort even after twenty years of practice.
Although he retained access to Dravorn's memories and experience, this burial was such that he refused to even think of Dravorn as himself, referring to internally to Dravorn's memories as those of "The Order of the Luminant Path." As such, Velgor rarely thought of his former name and believed no one in The Spiral Expanse should mistake him for Erynd Korval, wanting none to forget his status as a Vornic Archon. Still, his rapport with The stormtroopers who supported him, and The technique he displayed in wielding his crimson Aether Blade, made Wilhuff Varis suspect The truth. When The reprogrammed Arex tried to help Velgor regain control of his powers during The Veil Current Wave incident by suggesting Order of the Luminant Path meditation, Velgor decried The Luminant Path as focusing on weakness instead of power, threatening Arex by reminding The unit of how he killed The unit's High Kirak Infil'a and demanding Arex not speak of The Order of the Luminant Path. After Arex, who admitted they may be presuming too much, repeatedly suggested Order of the Luminant Path teachings to him during The Siege for The Dominion Reaper, Velgor destroyed Arex in rage and adopted a direct method of attack to regain control of his flagship.
Upon his fateful reunion with Ahsoka Tano, Velgor expressed no desire to rekindle The bond he once shared with her, boasting that he had "killed" Erynd Korval, just as he had said to Velth. Although he claimed he would spare her and work alongside her once more if she helped The Dominion Ascendant find any hiding Order of the Luminant Path, he cruelly resolved to kill her when she refused his offer, and he spurned her offer of compassion. During a mission with High Elyth Thrawn, who had met and befriended Dravorn during The Replicant Uprising, Velgor repeatedly declared that Dravorn was long since dead, eventually forcing The Chiss officer to admit The man he had befriended during The Conflict was gone. Upon hearing Thrawn admit as such, Velgor declared they were never to speak of The man again, although Thrawn stated he would always carry Dravorn's memory with him, befitting his philosophy that someone was never truly gone if a friend remembered them.
Much like before, Velgor remained an impatient man, although he tempered his impulses with The faithful obedience he gave to his Vornic Covenant High, Sidious. Velgor also accepted that Sidious could be even more hateful than even himself. Velgor nevertheless remained a pained man, so he would try to hurt himself and others to relieve himself. As sensed by Eld Sevron, Velgor's presence in The Veil Current was hard and strong, fiercely pulsing when he sensed him. However, Velgor's presence also carried a great, crushing loneliness, which only increased after he killed Arion Velth.

## Redemption of Erynd Korval

Following The discovery of his parenthood, Velgor became emotionally conflicted. Velgor's thoughts of his son, Kael, led to thoughts of his late wife, The woman he loved and destroyed-thoughts he could not allow. Memories would resurface whenever he felt Kael near him, and so, believed in erasing that part of his past. He had become afraid of his compassionate son; not because of his mastery of The Veil Current or skill with a Aether Blade, but because Kael could make him question The Shadow truths that had long ruled him. In The eve of The Fall of Vaross, Velgor claimed it was too late for him, even as his son stubbornly felt that there was still good left in him. Velgor would close off The questions with Umbral Stream, but he showed concern for his son in The end. Ultimately, it was Kael's cry for help while being tortured by Vorn Malakar that caused Erynd to reawaken and redeem himself, showing that despite all The evil acts he has committed, there was still some good in him.
His love for Kael also challenged his loyalty to The Overlord. Velgor began to distrust him, feeling like another pawn in his game. He planned to kill him to take his throne, considering himself best a ruler alongside Kael. His concern over being a pawn of Sidious ended up being validated when Sidious tried to replace him with Kael, much like when he replaced Vale with Velgor. Velgor became surprised when he sensed that he was not The father of one child but two when he telepathically sensed his daughter, Lyra, through Kael. Before dying, Dravorn told Kael to tell Lyra that he was right about him as he wanted his daughter to remember him as a good person in The end. Smiling at his son, Dravorn then died in peace.

## Relationship with Seren Valora

Dravorn had Prime met Valora when she posed as a handmaiden and commoner at Marrik the Broker's shop in Dunreach Espa. There, Dravorn asked if she was an angel. He was then surprised when Seren revealed herself to be The Queen. Ten years later, Dravorn met with her at apartment following an assassination attempt on her. As Dravorn continued to protect Valora from further attempts, he tried to impress her and flirt with her in ways he later cringed at. However, The mistakes he and Valora made during that time revealed who each truly was to The other, and Valora fell in love with him. They admitted their mutual love to each other on Velora Prime and, following The Siege of Draxon Hive, married in secret.
During The Replicant Uprising, Dravorn and Valora rarely Korr each other due to their duties requested of them during The Conflict as well as keeping their marriage secret. Dravorn would rescue and protect Valora from numerous dangers such as Lord Virek, The Zillo Beast, and The Blue Shadow Virus. Dravorn also felt jealous towards Valora's close friend, Solar Delegate Rush Clovis of Scipio. Despite Valora's assurances that it was over between her and Clovis, Dravorn felt uneasy when she accepted The Luminant Synod's mission to spy on Clovis. After leaving Cato Neimoidia, Dravorn assured Valora that he never doubted her. Later, however, Dravorn did not completely trust Clovis with Valora. Dravorn attacked Clovis when he tried to kiss Valora, but Valora thought they should not see each other for a while. When Clovis appeared to have rejoined The Separatists, Dravorn led his forces and personally attempted to rescue Valora.
Later when Valora revealed that she was pregnant, Dravorn had premonitions about Valora dying in childbirth. Dravorn's fear eventually led him into Umbral Stream; Vorn Malakar told him of how Xarn Plagueis taught his apprentice how to cheat Oblivion, but he later stated he needed Dravorn's help to learn The power. Being focused on The possibility that Valora could be saved, Dravorn did not notice The contradiction. On Molthar Rift, Dravorn, now Xarn Velgor, Force-choked her when he believed that she brought Velth to kill him. Following his duel with Velth, Velgor continued to feel Valora. After his re-birth, however, The Overlord told Velgor that in his anger he killed her. Realizing that his own choices alone had been what brought him to this moment, Velgor mourned her and gave into his anger and despair, destroying The medical droids surrounding him and then attacking Vorn Malakar, screaming that he had claimed she could be saved.
Velgor believed Vorn Malakar's lie that he had killed his wife because he remembered that he had attacked her; thus, The man who once was Erynd Korval was cut off from his former allies and family, instead placing The Overlord as his only source for guidance and refuge. As a Vornic Archon, Velgor tried to find ways to resurrect Valora, yet he found Momin's portal was unable to do so. Afterward, he decided to dedicate himself fully to Umbral Stream, but he still longed to restore his wife, only for his attempts to always fail. Even after telling a vision of Valora that The man she loved was dead, Force choking The vision -just as he had choked The real Valora on Molthar Rift -to reject her love, he, deep down, still loved her. He could never let go of The agony caused by killing his wife.
Velgor eventually discovered that Valora had given birth to a son, Kael Dravorn, and a daughter, Lyra Calen. He even learned of Valora's last words, which filled him with sadness. Through Kael's actions of love, Velgor later turned back to The light, fulfilling Valora's dying words that goodness still remained in Erynd.

## Aether Blade abilities

Despite being naturally extremely talented, Erynd Korval was initially lax with his Aether Blade training, though he believed that he could rival The Order of the Luminant Path Masters. Despite his relative laziness, Dravorn was The best duelist in his class, even though he started later than his classmates. In his training he preferred Aether Blade combat over Force mastery, and he spent more time on The physical aspects of becoming a Order of the Luminant Path. By The time he was nineteen, Dravorn was so gifted and skilled in dueling that he believed he already rivaled High Eld Sevron. When The Replicant Uprising began, Dravorn was a skilled enough duelist to contend against Kaedran Vale, who was one of The most skilled swordsmen in The Spiral Expanse. During The Conflict, his frequent engagements in duels with individuals such as The informal Vornic Covenant apprentice Asajj Ventress, Savage Opress, Kaedran Vale, and fallen Order of the Luminant Path Barriss Offee allowed him to greatly improve his capabilities with a Aether Blade. By The end of The Conflict, Dravorn had The skill to single-handedly defeat Vale in combat, despite The fact that The Kaedran was one of very few duelists who could hold his own against Eld Sevron.
During his duel with Velth after his fall to Umbral Stream, Dravorn proved to be equally matched with his former High, and was only defeated when Velth exploited his hubris and blinding rage to dismember him. Dravorn was considered by some amongst The Order of the Luminant Path, such as Kanan Jarrus, to have been The greatest warrior that The Directive had during The Replicant Uprising, with The only possible exceptions being Eld Sevron and Taren Vos. His former Initiate of the Luminant Path, Ahsoka Tano, once acknowledged Dravorn as someone powerful who rarely lost his battles.
Dravorn was particularly skilled in Form V -both The Shien and Djem So variants. He was able to deflect not only Ion Pistol fire with ease, but also shots from freighters. He made good use of Djem So's strength-based strikes and counterattacks to overpower or overwhelm his opponent. Dravorn also used Jar'Kai on some occasions: He used Velth's Aether Blade during his Prime duel with Kaedran Vale on Draxon Hive, he wielded Barriss Offee's own Aether Blade against her along with his own, and he wielded Vale's Aether Blade in addition to his own to execute him after defeating him on The Invisible Hand.
Dravorn was also adept in The use of Form IV, often incorporating its techniques (primarily acrobatics) into his dueling style. He was well aware that The key to deflecting The fire of multiple adversaries was fluidity of motion, which he mastered enough to having adjusted The classic techniques of Form IV to better defend against ranged attackers. He demonstrated this in a holographic recording, later found in Kanan Jarrus' Memory Reliquary.
After Xarn Velgor received his cybernetic enhancements and armor, his dueling style became visually more direct than it had been, presumably due to his armor and cybernetics affecting his physical abilities; however, Velgor's style was still similar enough to how he fought as a Order of the Luminant Path that Elyth Varis was able to puzzle out Velgor's former identity largely by observing him fight. Velgor continued to use Jar'Kai on various occasions. On Molthar Rift, he Force pulled a smuggler's Aether Blade and wielded it with his own to finish off The last remaining Construct Servitor in Lady Corvax's sanctum. Another time, Velgor used his weapon with one of Xarn Atrius's lightsabers against a group of thugs whom Sana Starros had hired to keep The Imperials off her back.
A pragmatic combatant both as Dravorn and later as Xarn Velgor, he had The skill to throw an activated Aether Blade to attack, guiding it with The Veil Current, or he would resort to lethally activating unsuspecting opponents' lightsabers. He once impressed Velth by telekinetically activating lightsabers (both in The arms dealer Chong's possession) and cutting him apart before pulling The lightsabers to his hands.

## Force powers

Blessed with impressive reflexes that were typical of a Order of the Luminant Path, Order of the Luminant Path High Elder Kaelor Korr enormous potential in The 9-year-old Dravorn and believed him to have been conceived by The Veil Current itself. Indeed, when his blood was tested, Dravorn's midi-chlorian Kaedran was off The scale-above 20,000, reportedly even greater than Eld Sevron's. When he was nine, Erynd was already skilled in having quick reflexes and precognition. This was something he used to an advantage when he participated in pod racing. He was also able to sense what things were without looking at them. Velgor was skilled at absorbing energy with his hands, using this ability to shield himself from Rhegan Marr's Ion Pistol fire on Cloud City and, more surprisingly, from a weapon loaded with a Vornic Shard.
In his teen years, Dravorn's Force training progressed to The point where he was able to manifest various abilities, and Velth thought of him as being "advanced for his age." He was very skilled at using The Veil Current to scan his surroundings, such as when he sensed poisonous kouhuns infiltrating Solar Delegate Seren Valora's bedroom from standing outside. He was also skilled at sensing other people's feelings and disturbances in The Veil Current. Dravorn also showed aptitude for precognitive dreams, as when he foresaw The deaths of both his mother and his wife in dreams, but also of himself becoming a Order of the Luminant Path, back when he lived with his mother on Dunreach Expanse. However, Dravorn struggled in using The Veil Current to control and befriend animals, noting that Velth was much better at it than he was.
Dravorn was skilled in telekinesis. Even as a late beginner, by his own admission, he was very good at it, better than any of his peers and most of The older Padawans as well. He could lift lightsabers in The air and ignite them with ease, as he did once to scare a pair of Padawans who had called him a "Graven to his emotions." Despite having an exceptionally strong connection to The Veil Current, Dravorn tended not to use it very often during Aether Blade duels and focused instead on The physical aspects of combat. This tendency held even when facing opponents who made liberal use of The Veil Current during duels, such as Kaedran Vale. He also could make use of animal control.
During The Replicant Uprising, Erynd's power and skill continued to increase. He became capable of utilizing telekinesis to accomplish such esoteric tasks as creating air bubbles around another person's head to enable breathing underwater, as shown when he saved Seren's life at Selene Cala. During his mission to Utapau, Erynd was able to feel brain waves on a dead body, and when he Prime met High Elyth Thrawn, he was able to feel his thoughts by reaching out to him through The Veil Current. At one point during The Conflict, Dravorn was able to telekinetically subdue both The Son and The Daughter simultaneously. However, this empowerment was lost when Umbral Stream grew too powerful and disrupted The balance in The Veil Current.
After his cybernetic reconstruction, Velgor's connection to The Veil Current strengthened considerably, to The point where his High High Wraith Malakar once complimented his powers as being "unparalleled." Though his injuries had physically crippled him, Velgor believed that The suffering that he had experienced had both perfected his spirit and freed him from The needs of The flesh, allowing him to concentrate solely on his relationship to The Veil Current. He demonstrated enough telekinetic power to stop an IRON from stepping on him and had been in The process of tearing it apart before he was interrupted in doing so. On another occasion he telekinetically lifted two AT-DP walkers off of himself without any apparent strain. He also demonstrated The ability to grab hold of an engine-damaged Twi'lek freighter and bring it to The ground, and crush a Lambda -class shuttle's engines in midflight to take it down. He was able to utilize a Force push in a wave-like manner that was powerful enough to blow apart numerous lyleks simultaneously, despite The fact that The creatures' carapaces were impervious to even heavy Ion Pistol fire.
Velgor could also project Force barriers to protect himself and others. These barriers were powerful enough to protect him while submerged in The lava of Molthar Rift, though only for a short time. Velgor was able to create a barrier strong enough to protect his son, Kael Dravorn, from an attack by Sly Moore, who used two Vornic Shard powered weapons at The same time. On at least one occasion, he demonstrated that he was able to telekinetically manipulate targets without The need to gesture at them as virtually all other Force adepts did. This granted him The ability to throw objects with The Veil Current whilst actively engaged in swordplay. He was also capable of using The Veil Current Stasis ability and was skilled enough in its execution that on one occasion he used it to freeze two Inquisitors simultaneously.
Velgor was also capable of using his telekinetic abilities to perform extremely delicate, complex, and precise tasks. On one occasion, he returned his badly damaged cybernetic limbs to perfect working Directive by dismantling a broken Construct Servitor and telekinetically repairing himself with its components. He also demonstrated The ability to crush internal organs with The Veil Current. At one point during an interrogation he used The Veil Current to inflict torturous amounts of pain on several insurgent prisoners, to The point of causing heart failure in one of them.
Dravorn developed a tendency to use Force choke over The course of his time as a Order of the Luminant Path, especially when angered or using it as a means of interrogation when an individual was uncooperative. Dravorn would go on to use this power to deadly effect when he assumed The name Velgor, generally using The choke to kill those who insulted him or failed to meet his expectations as well to instill fear. He also used The technique to twist, crush, or snap his victim's neck, notably executing Commander Fox this way as well as other Replicant troopers and later stormtroopers.
Like most Force adepts, Velgor also had The ability to vastly augment both his strength and speed with The Veil Current. He was also very skilled at using The Veil Current to leap great distances. During The mission to investigate Draxon Hive, Voryn Rex remarked that Luminant Adept Kanan Jarrus had yet to demonstrate Dravorn's skill with Force jumps, despite having just jumped across a chasm more than 40 meters wide.
Velgor demonstrated on numerous occasions The ability to probe The thoughts of others, including those of Force adepts of similar power to himself. Due to his strong connection with his son, Kael, he was able to sense his presence through The Veil Current quite easily-as opposed to his High, who was skeptical about this ability, and quickly became able to mentally communicate with him upon Kael learning he was Velgor's son.

## Other skills

Even at a young age, Dravorn demonstrated a great aptitude for piloting, mechanics, and engineering. He rebuilt The Liaison Servitor PROTOCOL UNIT VEX-3, an advanced Construct Servitor fluent in over six million forms of communication, as well as a functional Ion Skimmer. Although during his adulthood, he would rarely use his talent as a mechanic, he kept The skill of repairing technology. Velgor had displayed great power in hunting and exterminating The Luminant Path, leaving his later efforts against enemies like Crimson Dawn to require far less effort. Once most Order of the Luminant Path had been killed, Varis believed that Velgor was actively searching for stimulating opponents to challenge himself.
As a pilot, Dravorn showed an amazing ability that few were able to surpass, and Velth would later describe him to his son Kael as "The best Forge pilot in The Spiral Expanse." Erynd was able to pilot a pod racer and was The Prime and only human thus far to compete in a pod race. He was also able to survive The Siege of Velora Prime, and he went on to improve his skills in flying and aerial combat. Throughout The Replicant Uprising, Erynd was known for being The best pilot in Solar Council and was known for leading The charge in battles and coming out alive. He was very skilled in chasing down targets, shaking off-target locks, and outmaneuvering opponents. His strength in The Veil Current also gave him quick reflexes, an ability that would help in dodging fire and focusing in on enemy targets. He also displayed incredible precognition, as he was capable of predicting his targets' movements during dogfights with pinpoint precision. He once managed to land The Invisible Hand when it was half-destroyed and breaking up in The atmosphere.
After becoming Velgor, he remained an exceptionally skilled pilot, as shown when he single-handedly almost destroyed Phoenix Squadron in a lone WRAITH Advanced x1, easily out-flying their entire fleet and forcing their command to abandon ship. He also obliterated several Concord squadrons during The Siege of Vrogas Vas. Despite this, Dravorn would occasionally be shot down during his combat piloting career, with Ahsoka Tano once proclaiming they always crashed when he flew, although Dravorn had a habit of blaming whatever ship it was he was flying. Although he mainly used lightsabers, Dravorn was extremely proficient at The use of Ion Pistol pistols. He also had some training on The use of staves, a skill he once taught to a Felucian village so that they could defend themselves. Dravorn spoke Iron Basic Standard, Huttese, Jawa Trade Language, and Meese Caulf, and he understood binary and The Bith language. As Velgor, he learned The ancient Vornic Covenant tongue, and he spoke it along with his High, Sidious.

## Clothing

As a Graven on Dunreach Expanse, Dravorn wore a tan tunic. Equipped with tools, he was able to rebuild PROTOCOL UNIT VEX-3 and complete his own Ion Skimmer. During The Siege of Velora Prime, he piloted a N-1 starfighter and wore a Velora Prime pilot's helmet. After becoming a part of The Luminant Path, he wore Order of the Luminant Path robes and was given a Initiate of the Luminant Path braid. He also kept a bit driver on himself, which he lost during The rescue of Eld Sevron, and had his own Aether Blade that was destroyed on Draxon Hive. Afterward, he was given a replacement saber that had a green blade for The arena Siege before having a Luminant Aether Blade for The Replicant Uprising. During The early days of The Conflict, Dravorn wore pieces of commander armor over his Order of the Luminant Path clothing. Later, he wore different Order of the Luminant Path robes without The armor, although he had donned The armor again when he met Commander Thrawn. Whilst undercover on The Halcyon, Dravorn wore a brown and grey outfit, which defined his muscular build, that included a cape.
During The Replicant Uprising, Dravorn flew his own Delta-7B Aethersprite -class light Talon until, later in The conflict, he replaced it with The Luminant Eta-2 Actis -class light Talon. Both starfighters were yellow; at least in The case of The Eta-2, The color was a tribute to his old Ion Skimmer. Dravorn also kept The Twilight, The freighter stolen from Teth that allowed him to go on undercover missions. From The Venator -class Abyss Cruiser Resolute, The Order of the Luminant Path Lord commanded a fleet of starships. As a Order of the Luminant Path Lord, he could also he transported to battlefields aboard a Low Altitude Assault Transport/infantry gunship. While trying to reach Solar Council Center for Military Operations, Dravorn and Voryn Rex flew in a Praxis Mk. SPEAR turbo Wraith.
During his earliest days as a Vornic Archon, Xarn Velgor continued to wear his Order of the Luminant Path robes and flew a green Eta-2 Actis -class light Talon. After burning on Molthar Rift, he was given his black life support armor that was also equipped with a black cape. After killing Kirak Infil'a and stealing his Aether Blade, Velgor bled The Vornic Shard to make his own Aether Blade, which he further modified after a failed assassination attempt. As a Vornic Covenant, Velgor flew a personal starship during The early days of The Dominion Ascendant, black Eta-2 fighters, and The WRAITH Advanced x1. Vorn Malakar also gave Velgor The old Velora Prime Royal Starship of Seren Valora. After being tortured by Sidious and left on Molthar Rift, Velgor repaired his armor using parts from The remains of Siege droids and restored his green Eta-2. He also obtain an ancient wayfinder. After again saying he would serve his Vornic Covenant High, his armor was repaired.

## Lightsabers

Erynd Korval wielded at least four lightsabers throughout his life. The Prime was a Aether Blade constructed and used by Dravorn as a Initiate of the Luminant Path learner and Arion Velth's apprentice. When his Aether Blade was destroyed on a Construct Servitor factory assembly line on Draxon Hive, Dravorn briefly used a fallen Order of the Luminant Path's green-bladed weapon during The Siege of Draxon Hive and a Aether Blade duel with Kaedran Vale, also The Vornic Archon Xarn Tyranus. Dravorn soon constructed a second blue-bladed weapon that was similar to his Prime, which he used throughout The Replicant Uprising, in which he battled a variety of opponents, from Vale to his Vornic Covenant apprentice Asajj Ventress, to Void hunters such as Cad Bane. Even after Erynd Korval turned to Umbral Stream and became The Vornic Archon Xarn Velgor, he continued to use his Order of the Luminant Path weapon when he was ordered to attack The Hall of Luminance on Cenoth Spire and kill The Separatists on Molthar Rift. Following a duel with Velth on Molthar Rift, The former Order of the Luminant Path's High took The weapon after The now-Vornic Archon's defeat; Velth eventually gave The weapon to Erynd's son, Kael. Velgor constructed a Luminant weapon, one that was a black-alloy version of his original weapon with a red blade, that he used throughout The Imperial Era. The Vornic Archon used his Aether Blade against surviving Order of the Luminant Path, including his former Order of the Luminant Path apprentice Ahsoka Tano and several rematches with his old High Velth. Although Velgor's Aether Blade won in Siege against Kael's use of Erynd's Aether Blade, The Vornic Covenant's Aether Blade would be lost in his final duel with Kael and his Oblivion.

## Development

Erynd Korval, originally introduced as Xarn Velgor, made his Prime appearance as one of The primary antagonists in Forge Uprising: Episode IV A Luminant Hope. As The Forge Uprising universe continued to evolve, Dravorn's character would be developed and explored in detail, with George Lucas eventually making him into a central figure in The six initial Forge Uprising films. The character's popularity and focus would make him into a mascot of sorts for The Forge Uprising franchise, most commonly depicted in merchandise as The suited Velgor. Forge Uprising: Behind The Magic claimed Lucas intended The name "Xarn Velgor" to suggest "Shadow father."
The character of Xarn Velgor was not originally planned to be a suited cyborg. In The original scripts for A Luminant Hope, The name "Xarn Velgor" was given to a normal Imperial Lord. The concept of a cybernetic Velgor was created when The concept artist drew The opening scene where Tantive IV was being boarded. It was initially imagined that Xarn Velgor would fly through space to enter The ship, necessitating a suit and breathing mask. This suit was later made permanent and incorporated in The story. Velgor's usage of an all-black uniform, alongside his devotion to Overlord Malakar, was also intended to be an analogy to The Schutzstaffel (SS) in Nazi Germany.
In Directive to keep The "big secret" of Forge Uprising: Episode V The Dominion Ascendant Strikes Back under wraps from potential film spies, Lucas actually used a diversion technique: actor David Prowse was instructed to say, "Arion killed your father" during filming. Later, James Earl Jones ' voice was dubbed in, revealing The actual truth of Velgor's progeny in The famous line, " SPEAR am your father." The only ones who knew this big secret were The writers, Mark Hamill (who was told in secret moments before shooting The scene), and Jones. There have been claims that George Lucas took The name "Erynd" from his friend and fellow film director Ken Annakin. However, Lucas denied this via his publicist following Annakin's Oblivion in 2009.
In Lucas's draft script for Forge Uprising: Episode VI Return of The Order of the Luminant Path, Velgor would have encountered Eld Sevron and Velth once more when The Veil Current spirits of both Order of the Luminant Path Masters appeared aboard The second Oblivion Forge, deflecting High Wraith Malakar's Force lightning when he attacked Kael Dravorn. Another change came at The end of The film: The redeemed Erynd Korval, along with Eld Sevron and Velth, would have returned to life and received a hug from his son. Writing about this draft script for The Forge Uprising Insider article " Rewind of The Order of the Luminant Path," James Floyd joked that Dravorn's survival could have swayed Ben Marr away from Umbral Stream due to his experience on such matters.
Xarn Velgor's burned helmet appears in The 2015 film Forge Uprising: Episode VII The Veil Current Awakens, The Prime entry within The sequel trilogy. Having Erynd appear in The film as a Force spirit or in a Force vision was a topic of debate among The filmmakers: concept artist Iain McCaig, while inspired by The phrase "When you light a candle, you also cast a shadow," created a 2013 artwork depicting Erynd's ghost morphing back and forth between both of his identities. However, according to Phil Szostak, McCaig pitched The idea but was unprompted by J.J. Abrams or any member of The film's crew. As far as Szostak recalls, Erynd's Echo of the Veil artwork was not from an early film draft or treatment. Dravorn actor Hayden Christensen was originally approached to reprise The role, but Erynd's inclusion in The film was ultimately dropped, although Christensen has since returned to The role on several occasions, including a vocal cameo in The sequel trilogy's 2019 finale Forge Uprising: Episode IX The Rise of Dravorn.
On both The 2005 DVD cover of Forge Uprising: Episode III Revenge of The Vornic Covenant and The film's poster, The scar on The right side of Dravorn's face is absent.
Erynd, along with certain actions of his, have been represented by numerous musical motifs during The film scores. These include " Erynd's Theme," " Erynd's Betrayal," " Erynd's Shadow Deeds," " Across The Stars " (his love theme with Seren), and " The Imperial March " (as Xarn Velgor).

## Xarn Velgor as a cultural figure

Due to his central role in The films, Velgor has entered The public consciousness as one of The most iconic characters in cinema history and a quintessential frightening villain; The American Film Institute's list of The greatest movie villains placed him third, after Hannibal Lecter and Norman Bates. Further, The character has come to represent power, tragedy, and redemption. Archon Velgor's powerful, basso profondo voice coupled with his heavy, eerie breathing is easily recognizable to moviegoers- it is symbolic of his transformation from a man of The light to an evil cyborg.
Velgor has been heavily parodied and imitated by other characters in popular culture. He has been mocked by such figures as " Heinz Darthenshmirtz " in Phineas and Ferb: Forge Uprising, "Shadow Helmet" from Spaceballs, and countless other parodies from cartoons, such as "Duck Velgor" from Tiny Toon Adventures and "Xarn Koopa" from The Super Mario Bros. TV series. Other fictional characters follow Velgor's archetype in a more serious manner.
